## GHSA-8r5x-fm3f-whwj · handlebars

2026-10-08 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.8 · Pattern: `TYPE_CONFUSION→BYPASS`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from insufficient validation of Abstract Syntax Tree (AST) node properties within the Handlebars compiler. Specifically, an attacker could craft an AST with type-confused properties (e.g., a number where a string was expected for a path part, or an object for a literal value) that would bypass existing sanitization checks. This allowed the injection of arbitrary JavaScript into the compiled template output.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary JavaScript execution in the context of the application using Handlebars, leading to Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) or potentially more severe impacts depending on the application's environment.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/handlebars/compiler/compiler.js
+++ b/handlebars/compiler/compiler.js
@@ -288,41 +292,43 @@ Compiler.prototype = {
   },
 
   PathExpression: function(path) {
-    this.addDepth(path.depth);
-    this.opcode('getContext', path.depth);
+    const depth = validateDepth(path);
+    const parts = validateParts(path);
 
-    let name = path.parts[0],
+    this.addDepth(depth);
+    this.opcode('getContext', depth);
+
+    let name = parts[0],
       scoped = AST.helpers.scopedId(path),
-      blockParamId = !path.depth && !scoped && this.blockParamIndex(name);
+      blockParamId = !depth && !scoped && this.blockParamIndex(name);
 
     if (blockParamId) {
-      this.opcode('lookupBlockParam', blockParamId, path.parts);
+      this.opcode('lookupBlockParam', blockParamId, parts);
     } else if (!name) {
       // Context reference, i.e. `{{foo .}}` or `{{foo ..}}`
       this.opcode('pushContext');
     } else if (path.data) {
       this.options.data = true;
-      this.opcode('lookupData', path.depth, path.parts, path.strict);
+      this.opcode('lookupData', depth, parts, path.strict);
     } else {
-      this.opcode(
-        'lookupOnContext',
-        path.parts,
-        path.falsy,
-        path.strict,
-        scoped
-      );
+      this.opcode('lookupOnContext', parts, path.falsy, path.strict, scoped);
     }
   },
 
   StringLiteral: function(string) {
+    validateLiteral(string);
     this.opcode('pushString', string.value);
   },
 
   NumberLiteral: function(number) {
+    validateLiteral(number);
     this.opcode('pushLiteral', number.value);
   },
 
   BooleanLiteral: function(bool) {
+    validateLiteral(bool);
     this.opcode('pushLiteral', bool.value);
   },
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces comprehensive validation functions (e.g., `validateDepth`, `validateLiteral`, `validateParts`, `validateBlockParams`) that are called at various points in the compiler. These functions ensure that AST node properties conform to their expected types and formats before being processed, preventing type confusion and subsequent JavaScript injection.

## GHSA-r223-96jv-q533 · generator-jhipster

2026-10-08 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→SQL`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed in JHipster-generated reactive (WebFlux + R2DBC) applications where user-controlled input in the 'sort' parameter was directly incorporated into SQL queries without proper sanitization. This allowed attackers to inject arbitrary SQL code.

### Impact

An attacker could manipulate database queries, potentially leading to unauthorized data access, modification, or deletion, and could even execute arbitrary database commands.

### Key diff

```
--- generators/spring-boot/generators/data-relational/templates/src/main/java/_package_/repository/UserSqlHelper_reactive.java.ejs
+++ /dev/null
@@ -1,49 +0,0 @@
-<%#
- Copyright 2013-2026 the original author or authors from the JHipster project.
-...
-public class UserSqlHelper {
-
-    public static List<Expression> getColumns(Table table, String columnPrefix) {
-        List<Expression> columns = new ArrayList<>();
-        columns.add(Column.aliased("id", table, columnPrefix + "_id"));
-        columns.add(Column.aliased("login", table, columnPrefix + "_login"));
-<%_ if (!authenticationTypeOauth2) { _%>
-        columns.add(Column.aliased("password_hash", table, columnPrefix + "_password"));
-<%_ } _%>
-        columns.add(Column.aliased("first_name", table, columnPrefix + "_first_name"));
-        columns.add(Column.aliased("last_name", table, columnPrefix + "_last_name"));
-        columns.add(Column.aliased("email", table, columnPrefix + "_email"));
-        columns.add(Column.aliased("activated", table, columnPrefix + "_activated"));
-        columns.add(Column.aliased("lang_key", table, columnPrefix + "_lang_key"));
-        columns.add(Column.aliased("image_url", table, columnPrefix + "_image_url"));
-<%_ if (!authenticationTypeOauth2) { _%>
-        columns.add(Column.aliased("activation_key", table, columnPrefix + "_activation_key"));
-        columns.add(Column.aliased("reset_key", table, columnPrefix + "_reset_key"));
-        columns.add(Column.aliased("reset_date", table, columnPrefix + "_reset_date"));
-<%_ } _%>
-        return columns;
-    }
-}

--- /dev/null
+++ b/generators/spring-boot/generators/data-relational/templates/src/main/java/_package_/repository/AbstractR2dbcRepository_reactive.java.ejs
@@ -0,0 +1,320 @@
+<%#
+ Copyright 2013-2026 the original author or authors from the JHipster project.
+...
+public abstract class AbstractR2dbcRepository<T, ID> extends SimpleR2dbcRepository<T, ID> {
+...
+    /**
+     * Maps the sort properties to the columns of {@link #selectWithRelationsSql()}.
+     */
+    protected Map<String, String> sortColumns() {
+        retur
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the `UserSqlHelper_reactive.java.ejs` file and introduces `AbstractR2dbcRepository_reactive.java.ejs`. This new abstract repository provides a more robust way to handle sorting by mapping sort properties to columns and using parameterized queries, preventing direct injection of user input into SQL.

## GHSA-2gpf-2492-q9jh · praisonai

2026-10-08 · Python · **HIGH** 8.2 · Pattern: `INSECURE_DEFAULT→CONFIG`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an insecure default JWT secret and insufficient validation of the `CALL_SERVER_TOKEN`. In development environments, a default, well-known JWT secret was used, and in production, if not explicitly set, an ephemeral secret was generated with a warning. This allowed an attacker to potentially forge JWTs or bypass authentication if they knew the default secret or if the ephemeral secret was compromised.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass authentication for certain API endpoints, potentially gaining unauthorized access to sensitive operations or data within the PraisonAI platform.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/praisonai-platform/praisonai_platform/services/auth_service.py
+++ b/src/praisonai-platform/praisonai_platform/services/auth_service.py
-JWT_SECRET = os.environ.get("PLATFORM_JWT_SECRET")
+JWT_SECRET = resolve_jwt_secret()
-if JWT_SECRET is None or JWT_SECRET == _DEFAULT_SECRET:
-    # Determine if we are in an explicit dev environment.
-    # Only trust PLATFORM_ENV when it is explicitly set — an unset value
-    # must NOT silently fall back to "dev" because production deployments
-    # that forget the variable would run with a well-known signing key.
-    _explicit_env = os.environ.get("PLATFORM_ENV")
-    if _explicit_env == "dev":
-        # Developer explicitly opted in to insecure defaults — allow it.
-        JWT_SECRET = _DEFAULT_SECRET
-    else:
-        # Either production or environment not specified.
-        # Auto-generate an ephemeral secret so the service can start, but
-        # warn loudly because tokens will not survive a restart.
-        import secrets as _secrets
-        import warnings as _warnings
-
-        JWT_SECRET = _secrets.token_urlsafe(32)
-        _warnings.warn(
-            "PLATFORM_JWT_SECRET is not set. Auto-generated an ephemeral "
-            "signing key — JWT tokens will be invalidated on restart. "
-            "Set PLATFORM_JWT_SECRET to a stable random value for production "
-            "use, or set PLATFORM_ENV=dev to use the development default.",
-            stacklevel=2,
-        )
+--- a/src/praisonai/praisonai/api/call.py
+++ b/src/praisonai/praisonai/api/call.py
-    if CALL_SERVER_TOKEN:
-        token = request.query_params.get("token")
-        if not token:
-            auth = request.headers.get("Authorization", "")
-            if auth.startswith("Bearer "):
-                token = auth.split(" ")[1]
-            elif auth.startswith("Basic "):
-                try:
-                    import base64
-                    decoded = base64.b64decode(auth[6:]).decode("utf-8")
-                    if ":" in decoded:
-                        token = decoded.split(":", 1)[1]  # Use Password as token
-                    else:
-                        token = decoded
-                except Exception:
-                    pass
-        if token != CALL_SERVER_TOKEN:
-            raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED, detail="Unauthorized")
+    if not CALL_SERVER_TOKEN:
+        raise HTTPException(
+            status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,
+            detail="CALL_SERVER_TOKEN is not configured",
+        )
```

### Fix summary

The patch refactors JWT secret resolution into a dedicated service, `jwt_secret.py`. It introduces stricter checks for `PLATFORM_JWT_SECRET` in production environments, requiring it to be explicitly set and not equal to the default development secret. It also adds checks to ensure `CALL_SERVER_TOKEN` is configured before attempting authentication, preventing bypasses when it's unset.

## GHSA-9ffp-22j7-56r2 · react-jhipster

2026-10-08 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.6 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because JHipster-generated applications allowed users to upload and store files (blobs) with arbitrary content types. When these blobs were subsequently opened in a new browser window, the application used the user-provided content type directly to create a Blob URL. This allowed an attacker to upload a malicious HTML or SVG file with a content type like 'text/html' or 'image/svg+xml', which would then be rendered by the browser in the application's origin, leading to Same-Origin XSS.

### Impact

An attacker could inject and execute arbitrary client-side scripts in the context of the application's domain. This could lead to session hijacking, defacement, sensitive data theft, or further client-side attacks.

### Key diff

```
--- a/generators/client/generators/common/templates/src/main/webapp/app/shared/jhipster/data-utils.ts.ejs
+++ b/generators/client/generators/common/templates/src/main/webapp/app/shared/jhipster/data-utils.ts.ejs
@@ -46,9 +46,31 @@ export const toBase64 = (file: File, callback: (base64Data: string) => void): vo
   fileReader.readAsDataURL(file);
 }
 
-export const openFile = (data: string, contentType: string | null | undefined): void => {
-  contentType ??= '';
+export type BlobType = 'image' | 'text' | 'any';
+
+const DOWNLOAD_CONTENT_TYPE = 'application/octet-stream';
+
+/**
+ * Resolves the content type used to open a stored blob in a new window.
+ * Image blobs are opened as images, except XML based images like SVG which could execute scripts under the application origin.
+ * Text blobs are opened as plain text.
+ * Any other blob is opened as a download.
+ */
+export const toOpenableContentType = (contentType: string | null | undefined, blobType: BlobType = 'any'): string => {
+  const type = (contentType ?? '').split(';')[0].trim().toLowerCase();
+  if (type.endsWith('+xml')) {
+    return DOWNLOAD_CONTENT_TYPE;
+  }
+  if (blobType === 'image' && type.startsWith('image/') && !type.endsWith('+xml')) {
+    return type;
+  }
+  if (blobType === 'text' && type === 'text/plain') {
+    return type;
+  }
+  return DOWNLOAD_CONTENT_TYPE;
+};
+
+export const openFile = (data: string, contentType: string | null | undefined, blobType: BlobType = 'any'): void => {
   const byteCharacters = atob(data);
   const byteNumbers = new Array(byteCharacters.length);
   for (let i = 0; i < byteCharacters.length; i++) {
@@ -56,7 +98,7 @@ export const openFile = (data: string, contentType: string | null | undefined): 
   }1
   const byteArray = new Uint8Array(byteNumbers);
   const blob = new Blob([byteArray], {
-    type: contentType,
+    type: toOpenableContentType(contentType, blobType),
   });
   const fileURL = globalThis.URL.createObjectURL(blob);
   const win = globalThis.open(fileURL);
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `toOpenableContentType` function that sanitizes the content type before creating a Blob URL. It ensures that potentially dangerous content types (like HTML or XML-based images) are converted to 'application/octet-stream' to force a download, preventing them from being rendered in the browser. It also adds an optional `blobType` parameter to `openFile` to allow specifying if the blob is an 'image' or 'text', further refining the content type resolution.

## GHSA-h4xc-3qfq-jf93 · pydantic-ai-slim

2026-10-08 · Python · **HIGH** 7.6 · Pattern: `CSRF→STATE_CHANGE`

### Root cause

The web chat UI's API endpoint for handling chat requests (`/chat`) did not enforce a specific `Content-Type` header. This allowed cross-origin requests with 'CORS-safelisted' content types (like `text/plain`, `multipart/form-data`, `application/x-www-form-urlencoded`, or no content type) to bypass CORS preflight checks.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious website that, when visited by a developer running the Pydantic AI Web chat UI, would trigger agent runs and server-side tool execution on the developer's local machine. This could lead to arbitrary code execution or other harmful actions depending on the tools exposed by the agent.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pydantic_ai_slim/pydantic_ai/ui/_web/api.py
+++ b/pydantic_ai_slim/pydantic_ai/ui/_web/api.py
@@ -177,6 +202,15 @@ async def health(request: Request) -> Response:
 
     async def post_chat(request: Request) -> Response:
         """Handle chat requests via Vercel AI Adapter."""
+        if (media_type := request.headers.get('content-type', '').split(';')[0].strip().lower()) != JSON_MEDIA_TYPE:
+            return JSONResponse(
+                {'error': f'Expected `Content-Type: {JSON_MEDIA_TYPE}`, got {media_type or "no content type"}'},
+                status_code=415,
+            )
+
         adapter = await VercelAIAdapter[AgentDepsT, OutputDataT].from_request(
             request, agent=agent, sdk_version=sdk_version
         )
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a strict check for the `Content-Type` header on the `/chat` endpoint, requiring it to be exactly `application/json`. Since `application/json` is not a CORS-safelisted content type, any cross-origin request attempting to use it will trigger a CORS preflight. The `options_chat` handler is also updated to explicitly deny these preflight requests by not including `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` headers, effectively preventing cross-origin requests.

## GHSA-5x5g-h9x8-2fh9 · msgpack5

2026-10-08 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The streaming decoder processed only one message per `_transform` call. If multiple messages were buffered, it would recursively call `_transform` to process the remaining messages. This recursive behavior could lead to excessive stack depth if a large number of small messages were received, eventually causing a stack overflow.

### Impact

An attacker could send a specially crafted sequence of many small messages, causing the application to crash due to a stack overflow, leading to a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/streams.js
+++ b/lib/streams.js
@@ -64,26 +64,24 @@ Decoder.prototype._transform = function (buf, enc, done) {
     this._chunks.append(buf)
   }
 
-  try {
-    let result = this._msgpack.decode(this._chunks)
-    if (this._wrap) {
-      result = { value: result }
-    }
-    this.push(result)
-  } catch (err) {
-    if (err instanceof this._msgpack.IncompleteBufferError) {
-      done()
-    } else {
-      this.emit('error', err)
-    }
-    return
+  while (this._chunks.length > 0) {
+    try {
+      let result = this._msgpack.decode(this._chunks)
+      if (this._wrap) {
+        result = { value: result }
+      }
+      this.push(result)
+    } catch (err) {
+      if (err instanceof this._msgpack.IncompleteBufferError) {
+        done()
+      } else {
+        this.emit('error', err)
+      }
+      return
+    }
   }
 
-  if (this._chunks.length > 0) {
-    this._transform(null, enc, done)
-  } else {
-    done()
-  }
+  done()
 }
```

### Fix summary

The patch changes the message processing logic from a recursive call to an iterative `while` loop. Instead of calling `_transform` again, it continuously attempts to decode messages from the internal buffer within the same `_transform` call until the buffer is empty or an incomplete message is encountered.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 214 occurrences**
> Languages: Go, Python, JavaScript, RUST, Java, C#, PHP, Ruby, Erlang
> → GHSA-h749-fxx7-pwpg

## GHSA-6gcq-wc29-5xf2 · github.com/corazawaf/coraza/v3

2026-10-08 · Go · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The `gjson.Valid` function, used to validate JSON input, recursively processes JSON structures without an inherent depth limit. While the `readJSON` function had an `argumentLimit` and `maxRecursion` for its own processing logic, these guards did not prevent `gjson.Valid` from being called on deeply nested JSON tails that were truncated by the argument/byte limits. This allowed an attacker to craft a JSON input with excessive nesting, leading to an unbounded recursion in `gjson.Valid`.

### Impact

An attacker could send a specially crafted, deeply nested JSON payload to the Coraza WAF, causing the `gjson.Valid` function to trigger a stack overflow. This would result in a process crash, leading to a denial of service for the Coraza WAF.

### Key diff

```
--- a/internal/bodyprocessors/json.go
+++ b/internal/bodyprocessors/json.go
@@ -136,6 +136,11 @@ func readJSON(s string, maxRecursion int, argumentLimit int) (res map[string][]s
 	if err != nil {
 		return res, truncated, err
 	}
+	// ... (comment explaining the issue)
+	if jsonNestingExceedsLimit(s, maxRecursion) {
+		return res, truncated, errors.New("max recursion reached while reading json object")
+	}
 	if !gjson.Valid(s) {
 		return res, truncated, errors.New("invalid JSON")
 	}
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new function, `jsonNestingExceedsLimit`, which iteratively (without recursion) checks if the JSON input's nesting depth exceeds a predefined `maxRecursion` limit. This check is performed before calling `gjson.Valid`. If the nesting limit is exceeded, an error is returned, preventing `gjson.Valid` from processing the malicious input and thus mitigating the stack overflow vulnerability.

## GHSA-77xj-x4rm-935c · datamodel-code-generator

2026-10-08 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The application was vulnerable to path traversal due to insufficient validation of user-controlled import paths in Protobuf files. When processing 'weak-import' statements, the `import_path` was directly appended to a temporary directory path without canonicalization or checks, allowing an attacker to specify paths like `../../` to write files outside the intended temporary directory.

### Impact

An attacker could write arbitrary files to arbitrary locations on the file system where the application has write permissions, potentially leading to remote code execution or denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/datamodel_code_generator/parser/protobuf.py
+++ b/src/datamodel_code_generator/parser/protobuf.py
@@ -382,13 +387,17 @@ def _write_sanitized_file(self, source_path: Path, temp_path: Path, root: Path)
 
     def _write_missing_weak_imports(self, input_files: Iterable[Path], include_paths: Sequence[Path]) -> None:
         assert self.weak_import_dir is not None
+        weak_import_dir = self.weak_import_dir.resolve()
         for input_file in input_files:
             text = input_file.read_text(encoding=self.parser.encoding)
             syntax = 'syntax = "proto3";\n' if 'syntax = "proto3"' in text else 'syntax = "proto2";\n'
             for import_path in WEAK_IMPORT_PATTERN.findall(text):
+                stub = (weak_import_dir / import_path).resolve()
+                if not stub.is_relative_to(weak_import_dir):
+                    msg = f"Invalid Protocol Buffers weak import path: {import_path!r}"
+                    raise SchemaParseError(msg)
                 if any((include_path / import_path).exists() for include_path in include_paths):
                     continue
-                stub = self.weak_import_dir / import_path
                 stub.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
                 stub.write_text(syntax, encoding=self.parser.encoding)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a check to ensure that the resolved path for a weak import (`stub`) remains within the designated `weak_import_dir`. It canonicalizes both the `weak_import_dir` and the `stub` path using `.resolve()` and then verifies that `stub` is a relative path to `weak_import_dir` using `is_relative_to()`. Additionally, a `try...except` block was added to ensure the temporary directory is cleaned up in case of an error during initialization.

## GHSA-8f34-f56x-9xph · msgpack5

2026-10-08 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The msgpack5 library's decoder did not correctly define the header sizes for all MessagePack types, specifically 'map32'. When encountering a truncated 'map32' header, the decoder would attempt to read beyond the buffer's bounds, leading to an unexpected error and application crash.

### Impact

An attacker could provide specially crafted, truncated MessagePack data to a service using this library, causing the service to crash and resulting in a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/decoder.js
+++ b/lib/decoder.js
@@ -28,9 +28,10 @@ const SIZES = {
   0xd9: 2,
   0xda: 3,
   0xdb: 5,
-  0xde: 3,
   0xdc: 3,
-  0xdd: 5
+  0xdd: 5,
+  0xde: 3,
+  0xdf: 5
 }
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds the missing '0xdf' entry to the SIZES constant, which corresponds to the 'map32' MessagePack type, and correctly assigns it a header size of 5 bytes. This ensures the decoder correctly handles 'map32' headers, preventing out-of-bounds reads.

## GHSA-jw42-f3rr-4cc3 · github.com/xuri/excelize/v2

2026-10-08 · Go · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→INFINITE_LOOP`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the Excelize library did not validate the row number attribute ('r') when parsing XML data from an Excel file. An attacker could craft an Excel file with an extremely large row number, causing the `GetRows` and `Rows` iterator functions to attempt to process an unbounded number of rows.

### Impact

An attacker could provide a malicious Excel file that, when processed by an application using the Excelize library, would lead to an infinite loop or an extremely long processing time, effectively causing a denial of service for the application.

### Key diff

```
--- a/rows.go
+++ b/rows.go
@@ -176,7 +179,10 @@ func (rows *Rows) Columns(opts ...Options) ([]string, error) {
 			rowIterator.inElement = xmlElement.Name.Local
 			if rowIterator.inElement == "row" {
 				rowNum := 0
-				if rowNum, rowIterator.err = attrValToInt("r", xmlElement.Attr); rowNum != 0 {
+				if rowNum, rowIterator.err = attrValToInt("r", xmlElement.Attr); rowNum > TotalRows {
+					rows.err, rows.token = ErrMaxRows, nil
+					return rowIterator.cells, rows.err
+				} else if rowNum != 0 {
 					rows.curRow = rowNum
 				} else if rows.token == nil {
 					rows.curRow++
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a check to ensure that the parsed row number does not exceed a predefined maximum value (`TotalRows`). If an excessively large row number is encountered, an error (`ErrMaxRows`) is returned, preventing the unbounded loop. Additionally, a check for existing errors was added to the `Next()` method to prevent further processing if an error has already occurred.

## GHSA-x2q3-8cjh-766f · github.com/xuri/excelize/v2

2026-10-08 · Go · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The application was vulnerable to a DoS attack because it allocated buffers for 'EncryptionInfo' and 'EncryptedPackage' streams based on attacker-controlled sizes from CFB directory entries without proper validation. This could lead to the allocation of excessively large or negatively sized buffers, causing a panic or out-of-memory error.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious Excel file that, when processed by the application, would cause it to crash or consume all available memory, leading to a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/crypt.go
+++ b/crypt.go
@@ -190,22 +194,24 @@ func Encrypt(raw []byte, opts *Options) ([]byte, error) {
 }
 
 // extractPart extract data from storage by specified part name.
-func extractPart(doc *mscfb.Reader) ([]byte, []byte, error) {
+func extractPart(doc *mscfb.Reader, maxStreamSize int64) ([]byte, []byte, error) {
 	var encryptionInfoBuf, encryptedPackageBuf []byte
 	for entry, err := doc.Next(); err == nil; entry, err = doc.Next() {
 		switch entry.Name {
-		case "EncryptionInfo":
-			buf := make([]byte, entry.Size)
-			if _, err := doc.Read(buf); err != nil {
-				return encryptionInfoBuf, encryptedPackageBuf, err
+		case "EncryptionInfo", "EncryptedPackage":
+			if entry.Size < 0 || maxStreamSize < entry.Size {
+				return encryptionInfoBuf, encryptedPackageBuf, ErrWorkbookFileFormat
 			}
-			encryptionInfoBuf = buf
-		case "EncryptedPackage":
 			buf := make([]byte, entry.Size)
 			if _, err := doc.Read(buf); err != nil {
 				return encryptionInfoBuf, encryptedPackageBuf, err
 			}
-			encryptedPackageBuf = buf
+			if entry.Name == "EncryptionInfo" {
+				encryptionInfoBuf = buf
+			}
+			if entry.Name == "EncryptedPackage" {
+				encryptedPackageBuf = buf
+			}
 		}
 	}
 	return encryptionInfoBuf, encryptedPackageBuf, nil
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces size validation for 'EncryptionInfo' and 'EncryptedPackage' streams. It now checks if the entry size is negative or exceeds a newly defined maximum stream size (MaxCFBStreamSizeV3 or MaxCFBStreamSizeV4, depending on the file version) before allocating the buffer. If the size is invalid, an error is returned.

## GHSA-x8v2-478q-2hvg · org.asynchttpclient:async-http-client

2026-10-08 · Java · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The AsyncHttpClient library, when using WebSocket permessage-deflate compression, did not impose a limit on the size to which a compressed message could decompress. An attacker could send a small, highly compressed WebSocket message that would expand into a very large amount of data, consuming excessive memory and CPU resources.

### Impact

An attacker could trigger a decompression bomb, leading to a denial of service by exhausting the server's memory and CPU, making the application unresponsive or crashing it.

### Key diff

```
--- a/client/src/main/java/org/asynchttpclient/netty/channel/ChannelManager.java
+++ b/client/src/main/java/org/asynchttpclient/netty/channel/ChannelManager.java
@@ -149,6 +149,9 @@ public class ChannelManager {
     private final @Nullable AddressResolverGroup<InetSocketAddress> addressResolverGroup;
 
     private final ChannelPool channelPool;
+    // Bounded, unlike WebSocketClientCompressionHandler.INSTANCE, since the frame and buffer limits only
+    // bound compressed bytes. @Sharable, so one instance serves every channel.
+    private final WebSocketClientCompressionHandler webSocketCompressionHandler;
     private final ChannelGroup openChannels;
     // HTTP/2 registry, grouped by per-host base key so a permit-starved round-robin request can find a
     // sibling-IP connection (issue #2214). Outer key: the per-host base partition key. Inner key: the full
@@ -193,6 +196,7 @@ private boolean isIoType(IoEventLoopGroup eventLoopGroup, String ioHandlerClassN
 
     public ChannelManager(final AsyncHttpClientConfig config, Timer nettyTimer) {
         this.config = config;
+        webSocketCompressionHandler = new WebSocketClientCompressionHandler(config.getWebSocketMaxDecompressedFrameSize());
 
         sslEngineFactory = config.getSslEngineFactory() != null ? config.getSslEngineFactory() : new DefaultSslEngineFactory();
         try {
@@ -448,7 +452,7 @@ protected void initChannel(Channel ch) {
                         .addLast(AHC_WS_HANDLER, wsHandler);
 
                 if (config.isEnableWebSocketCompression()) {
-                    pipeline.addBefore(AHC_WS_HANDLER, WS_COMPRESSOR_HANDLER, WebSocketClientCompressionHandler.INSTANCE);
+                    pipeline.addBefore(AHC_WS_HANDLER, WS_COMPRESSOR_HANDLER, webSocketCompressionHandler);
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new configuration option, `webSocketMaxDecompressedFrameSize`, which sets a maximum limit on the size a permessage-deflate WebSocket message can inflate to. This limit is now enforced by a new `WebSocketClientCompressionHandler` instance, preventing unbounded decompression.

## GHSA-v2j5-22fr-j62r · org.asynchttpclient:async-http-client

2026-10-08 · Java · **HIGH** 7.4 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The connection pool key for schemes like NTLM and Negotiate (which authenticate the connection itself, not each request) only included the principal, not other critical authentication parameters. This allowed connections authenticated under one set of credentials (e.g., domain, host, login context) to be reused by requests intended for a different set of credentials, as long as the principal was the same.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially reuse a pooled connection authenticated by a legitimate user to send requests as that user, even if their own credentials differ in other aspects (e.g., domain). This could lead to unauthorized access to resources or actions on behalf of another user.

### Key diff

```
--- a/client/src/main/java/org/asynchttpclient/netty/channel/PrincipalScopedPartitionKey.java
+++ b/client/src/main/java/org/asynchttpclient/netty/channel/PrincipalScopedPartitionKey.java
-        return new PrincipalScopedPartitionKey(baseKey, realm.getScheme(), realm.getPrincipal());
+        String identity = (originScoped ? identityOf(realm) : "") + '/' + (proxyScoped ? identityOf(proxyRealm) : "");
+        return new PrincipalScopedPartitionKey(baseKey, scheme, identity);
...
+    private static String identityOf(Realm realm) {
+        MessageDigest digest = DIGEST.get();
+        digest.reset();
+        update(digest, realm.getScheme().name());
+        update(digest, realm.getPrincipal());
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `PrincipalScopedPartitionKey` to include a cryptographic hash (SHA-256) of all relevant authentication parameters (scheme, principal, password, NTLM domain/host, login context, service principal name, realm name, canonical hostname setting, and custom login config) when generating the connection pool key. This ensures that connections are strictly partitioned based on the full authentication identity, preventing unintended sharing.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 78 occurrences**

> → GHSA-2679-6mx9-h9xc

## GHSA-q7m5-3jmv-vm48 · praisonai

2026-10-08 · Python · **MODERATE** 5.5 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `ContextGatherer` component, responsible for resolving include paths, did not properly validate or sanitize user-supplied paths. This allowed an attacker to use absolute paths or path traversal sequences (e.g., `../`) to access files outside the intended workspace.

### Impact

An attacker could read arbitrary files on the system where PraisonAI is running, potentially leading to information disclosure of sensitive data, configuration files, or source code.

### Key diff

```
--- src/praisonai/praisonai/cli/features/agent_scheduler.py.bak
+++ /dev/null
deleted file mode 100644
```

### Fix summary

The provided diff shows the deletion of the `agent_scheduler.py.bak` file, which is not directly related to the described vulnerability. The actual fix, as per the advisory, would involve implementing robust path validation and sanitization within the `ContextGatherer` to restrict file access to the designated workspace.

## GHSA-xw65-4hp5-5hc7 · handlebars

2026-10-08 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 4.7 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The Handlebars precompiler and runtime did not properly escape certain characters when embedding precompiled templates or string literals into generated JavaScript code. Specifically, it failed to escape HTML script delimiters like '<script' and '</script', as well as line/paragraph separators and lone surrogate characters, allowing an attacker to inject arbitrary JavaScript when user-controlled input was used in template names or other string contexts that were then embedded directly into the generated JavaScript.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary JavaScript execution in the context of the web page where the precompiled template is rendered. This could lead to Cross-Site Scripting (XSS), allowing session hijacking, defacement, or redirection to malicious sites.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/handlebars/compiler/code-gen.js
+++ b/lib/handlebars/compiler/code-gen.js
@@ -171,17 +171,16 @@ CodeGen.prototype = {
   },
 
   quotedString: function(str) {
-    return (
-      '"' +
-      (str + '')
-        .replace(/\\/g, '\\\\')
-        .replace(/"/g, '\"')
-        .replace(/\n/g, '\\n')
-        .replace(/\r/g, '\\r')
-        .replace(/\u2028/g, '\\u2028') // Per Ecma-262 7.3 + 7.8.4
-        .replace(/\u2029/g, '\\u2029') +
-      '"'
-    );
+    str = str + '';
+    if (!NEEDS_ESCAPING.test(str)) {
+      return '"' + str + '"';
+    }
+
+    return safeJsonLiteral(str);
   },
 
   objectLiteral: function(obj) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `safeJsonLiteral` function that extends `JSON.stringify` to additionally escape HTML script delimiters ('<script', '</script', '<!--'), line/paragraph separators (U+2028, U+2029), and lone UTF-16 surrogate code units. This new function is then used consistently across the codebase for all string literals embedded into generated JavaScript, ensuring proper escaping and preventing injection.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 127 occurrences**

## GHSA-jmqq-x5g9-9p2w · org.asynchttpclient:async-http-client

2026-10-08 · Java · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `CREDENTIAL_LEAK→LOG_EXPOSURE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an incomplete update of the `NettyResponseFuture` state during a request replay initiated by a `ResponseFilter`. When a filter replayed a request to a different host, especially one behind a proxy, the `proxyServer` and `basePartitionKeyCache` fields of the `NettyResponseFuture` were not correctly recomputed or updated. This led to a situation where a connection established through a proxy for the new target host was incorrectly filed in the connection pool under a direct connection key for that host.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially cause sensitive information, such as credentials (e.g., HTTP Basic Auth headers), intended for a direct connection to be sent through a proxy server. This could lead to the exposure of credentials to an unintended third party (the proxy) or allow an attacker to intercept them if they control the proxy.

### Key diff

```
--- a/client/src/main/java/org/asynchttpclient/netty/NettyResponseFuture.java
+++ b/client/src/main/java/org/asynchttpclient/netty/NettyResponseFuture.java
@@ -94,7 +94,9 @@ public final class NettyResponseFuture<V> implements ListenableFuture<V> {
     private final long start = unpreciseMillisTime();
     private final ChannelPoolPartitioning connectionPoolPartitioning;
     private final ConnectionSemaphore connectionSemaphore;
-    private final ProxyServer proxyServer;
+    // Not final: a filter replay can retarget this future at a different origin, reached through a
+    // different proxy or none at all. It feeds basePartitionKeyCache, so it has to move with the target.
+    private ProxyServer proxyServer;
     private final int maxRetry;
     private final CompletableFuture<V> future = new CompletableFuture<>();
     public Throwable pendingException;
```

### Fix summary

The patch makes the `proxyServer` field in `NettyResponseFuture` non-final and introduces a `setProxyServer` method that also recomputes the `basePartitionKeyCache`. In `NettyRequestSender.replayRequest`, it now explicitly updates the `proxyServer` and `targetRequest` on the future, ensuring the `basePartitionKeyCache` is correctly recomputed. It also clears `proxyRealm` and potentially `realm` if the base URI changes or a scheme downgrade occurs, and passes the `initialPartitionKey` to `drainChannelAndOffer` to correctly manage the channel in the pool.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 9 occurrences**
> Languages: Java, Go, Python, C#, JavaScript
> → GHSA-cmxv-58fp-fm3g
> → GHSA-pm7q-rjjx-979p
> → GHSA-f7j3-774f-rfhj
> → GHSA-vh4v-2xq2-g5cg
> → GHSA-q62h-354g-5r85
> → GHSA-pf56-329r-95rw
> → GHSA-p2f4-r6v6-j797
> → GHSA-6x2c-phff-wx57

🔗 [View commit](https://github.com/AsyncHttpClient/async-http-client/commit/15b254514a411623e5f1d8c99ea79c0f82f8a466)

## GHSA-mx22-3794-2vpv · github.com/xuri/excelize/v2

2026-10-08 · Go · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `NULL_DEREF→CRASH`

### Root cause

The application did not validate the 'fieldIdx' and 'Fld' values extracted from the pivot table definition against the bounds of the 'order' slice (pivot cache fields). An attacker could craft an Excel file with out-of-bounds indices, leading to a panic.

### Impact

An attacker could provide a specially crafted Excel file that, when processed, would cause the application to crash due to an out-of-bounds access, leading to a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pivotTable.go
+++ b/pivotTable.go
@@ -1427,6 +1427,9 @@ func (f *File) pivotCacheReader(path string) (*xlsxPivotCacheDefinition, error)
 func (f *File) extractPivotTableFields(pt *xlsxPivotTableDefinition, pc *xlsxPivotCacheDefinition, opts *PivotTableOptions) {
 	order := pc.getPivotCacheFieldsName()
 	for fieldIdx, field := range pt.PivotFields.PivotField {
+		if fieldIdx >= len(order) {
+			continue
+		}
 		if field.Axis == "axisRow" {
 			opts.Rows = append(opts.Rows, pc.extractPivotTableField(order[fieldIdx], field))
 		}
@@ -1439,6 +1442,9 @@ func (f *File) extractPivotTableFields(pt *xlsxPivotTableDefinition, pc *xlsxPiv
 	}
 	if pt.DataFields != nil {
 		for _, field := range pt.DataFields.DataField {
+			if field.Fld < 0 || field.Fld >= len(order) {
+				continue
+			}
 			dataField := PivotTableField{
 				Data:     order[field.Fld],
 				Name:     field.Name,
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds boundary checks before accessing elements in the 'order' slice. It ensures that 'fieldIdx' and 'Fld' are within the valid range of the 'order' slice, preventing out-of-bounds panics.

## GHSA-p8wg-vrv2-v86f · handlebars

2026-10-08 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `PROTOTYPE_POLLUTION→OVERRIDE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an incomplete prototype access control mechanism. Handlebars allowed direct access to 'own properties' of an object without further checks. This bypass was exploitable when an attacker could craft an object with a 'constructor' property that was considered an 'own property' of a prototype, leading to the exposure of dangerous constructors like `Function`.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary code execution within the context of the Handlebars application, potentially leading to full system compromise if the application runs with elevated privileges.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/handlebars/runtime.js
+++ b/lib/handlebars/runtime.js
@@ -144,7 +145,15 @@ export function template(templateSpec, env) {
       if (result == null) {
         return result;
       }
-      if (Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(parent, propertyName)) {
+      if (
+        Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(parent, propertyName) &&
+        !isPrototypeConstructor(parent, propertyName, result)
+      ) {
         return result;
       }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new function `isPrototypeConstructor` to specifically detect and block access to a prototype's own 'constructor' property. It modifies the `template` function to ensure that even 'own properties' are checked by `isPrototypeConstructor` before being returned, preventing the bypass of prototype access controls for dangerous constructors. Additionally, the `escapeExpression` function was hardened to only treat properties with `typeof string.toHTML === 'function'` as safe strings.

## GHSA-r7x5-4969-99p7 · github.com/xuri/excelize/v2

2026-10-08 · Go · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `NULL_DEREF→CRASH`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the code attempted to access the 'RID' field of 'ws.Drawing' without first checking if 'ws.Drawing' itself was nil. If a worksheet had an 'ExtLst' element but no 'Drawing' element, 'ws.Drawing' would be nil, leading to a nil-pointer dereference when 'ws.Drawing.RID' was accessed.

### Impact

An attacker could trigger a program crash (denial of service) by providing a specially crafted Excel file where a worksheet contains an 'extLst' element but lacks a 'drawing' element, causing the application to terminate unexpectedly.

### Key diff

```
--- a/slicer.go
+++ b/slicer.go
@@ -814,7 +814,7 @@ func (f *File) GetSlicers(sheet string) ([]SlicerOptions, error) {
 	if err != nil {
 		return slicers, err
 	}
-	if ws.ExtLst == nil {
+	if ws.ExtLst == nil || ws.Drawing == nil {
 		return slicers, err
 	}
 	target := f.getSheetRelationshipsTargetByID(sheet, ws.Drawing.RID)
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds an additional check to ensure that 'ws.Drawing' is not nil before attempting to access its 'RID' field. This prevents the nil-pointer dereference by short-circuiting the logic if the 'Drawing' element is absent.
